# Amadeus Session -1 (Encode, Decode & Amadeus Basic Commands) #gdstraining #amadeus #gds #jobs
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/DCkjPWlRLds
This video serves as an introductory session for the Amadeus Global Distribution System (GDS), a software used by travel professionals to book flights and manage travel data. The session focuses on understanding the concepts of encoding (getting a code from a name) and decoding (getting a name from a code).

Core Amadeus Commands
1. Country Encoding/Decoding (Command: DC)
This command provides details about a country, including its two-letter code, region, currency, and citizenship/passport code.

Example: DC INDIA returns the code IN, currency INR, and passport code IND (4:42).
Example: DC DE returns Germany, showing how the command works for decoding codes back into names (7:03).
2. Airline Encoding/Decoding (Command: DNA)
Used to retrieve information about airlines, including their IATA booking code and numeric ticketing code.

Example: DNA INDIGO returns the booking code 6E and ticketing code 312 (9:42).
Example: DNA 001 returns American Airlines, demonstrating how the command can identify an airline via its numeric ticketing code (11:29).
3. City and Airport Encoding/Decoding
These commands identify travel hubs and their associated infrastructure like terminals, railway stations, and bus stations.

Encoding (DAN): Use this when you have the city or airport name.
Example: DAN DELHI confirms the city code DEL and identifies the Indira Gandhi International Airport (12:12).
Decoding (DAC): Use this when you have the city or airport code.
Example: DAC BOM identifies Mumbai and provides details on the Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International Airport (15:37).
Example: DAC DXB provides a comprehensive list of all six airports and four bus stations serving Dubai (17:18).
Key Takeaways
Encoding is essential because while city names can be duplicate, GDS codes are unique, preventing booking errors (3:23).
The system categorizes locations with identifiers: A (Airport), C (City), R (Railway Station), H (Helipad), and B (Bus Station) (12:25).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session - 2 Command to convert time & currency, To check time of any city in Amadeus
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/qVK9CUrEF2k

This video serves as a guide for using basic Amadeus GDS (Global Distribution System) commands to handle time, calculations, and currency conversion without needing external tools.

1. Time Management Commands (DD)
The DD command allows users to check the current time in different cities or convert times between time zones.

Check City Time: Use DD followed by the city code.
Example: DDNYC to check the time in New York (1:22).
Convert Time: Use DD followed by the source city code, time, and target city code.
Example: DDDXB1200/BOM calculates what time it is in Mumbai (BOM) when it is 12:00 in Dubai (DXB) (2:28).
Example: DDMEL0700/DEL determines the time in Delhi (DEL) when it is 07:00 in Melbourne (MEL) (3:11).
2. Calculation Commands (DF)
The DF command acts as an internal calculator within Amadeus, useful for ticket fare calculations. Note: Use a semicolon (;) instead of a plus sign (+).

Addition: DF500;230 (4:51)
Subtraction: DF500-230 (5:29)
Multiplication: DF170*4 (6:02)
Division: DF800/5 (6:32)
3. Currency Conversion (FCC)
The FCC command is used to convert currency values based on current exchange rates.

Syntax: FCC + Amount + Currency Code + / + Target Currency Code.
Example: FCC100000AED/INR converts 100,000 UAE Dirham to Indian Rupees (8:30).
Example: FCCIINR/AUD converts 100,000 Indian Rupees to Australian Dollars (9:47).
These commands are essential for travel agents to perform quick operations directly within the Amadeus environment (10:11).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus session - 3 What is PNR? How to create a PNR? Mandatory elements to create a PNR? PRINT
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/ojJYs5ZTO5M
This video serves as the third session in an *Amadeus* GDS (Global Distribution System) training series, focusing on the fundamental concepts and creation of a PNR (**Passenger Name Record**). A PNR is a 6-character alphanumeric reference number generated by the system to store passenger information (0:16-0:34).

### **Mandatory PNR Elements**
To create a valid PNR, five specific elements are required. While the system can technically process the first four in any order, there is an ideal workflow (3:21-4:05):

1.  **Itinerary (0:16):** This involves two steps: checking flight availability and then selling the desired seat(s) (6:07-6:26).
2.  **Name Field (0:16):** Entering the passenger's name. The system categorizes passengers by type (PTC): 
    *   **Adult:** 12+ years old (8:33).
    *   **Child:** 2-12 years old (8:53).
    *   **Infant:** Under 2 years old (9:09).
3.  **Phone Field (0:16):** Used to record contact details (mobile or email) so airlines can notify passengers of schedule changes (4:20-5:30).
4.  **Time Limit (0:16):** The timeframe between booking a seat and issuing the ticket (6:33-7:03).
5.  **Receive From (0:16):** The final step, indicating who created the booking (e.g., agent initials) to finalize the PNR (5:33-5:55).

### **Cabin Classes and Identifiers**
When booking, users can select specific cabin classes using system identifiers (7:20-7:56):
*   **Economy:** Use `KM` (7:39).
*   **Premium Economy:** Use `KW` (7:45).
*   **Business:** Use `KC` (7:52).
*   **First Class:** Use `KF` (7:54).

### **Important Notes on PNRs**
*   **GDS vs. Airline PNR:** GDS PNRs (travel agency reference) are always 6 alphanumeric characters. Airline PNRs may vary in length or format depending on whether the airline uses the same software as the GDS (e.g., *British Airways* and *Air India* use *Amadeus*, meaning their PNRs are identical to the GDS PNR) (0:39-2:26).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# LIVE: How to create PNR in amadeus. All Amadeus Commands and steps. What is PRINT?
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/gjv2haCHtcA
This video serves as an introductory guide to creating a **Passenger Name Record (PNR)** in the *Amadeus* GDS system. To create a valid PNR, users must input five **mandatory elements**, often referred to by the mnemonic **PRINT** (Phone, Received from, Itinerary, Name, Ticketing/Time limit).

### Mandatory Elements and Commands

1. **Itinerary (0:29-5:51):** 
   - **Availability Check:** Use the command `AN[Date][Origin][Destination]` (e.g., `AN21MARPARLON`) to view flight options. You can filter by airline (e.g., `A6X`) (1:26).
   - **Seat Selection (SS):** Once a flight is chosen, use `SS1[Class][LineNumber]` to sell a seat (e.g., `SS1G3` sells one seat in G class on the third line of the availability display) (5:19).

2. **Name Field (5:56-6:38):** 
   - Use the command `NM1[LastName]/[FirstName][Title]` to add the passenger. Example: `NM1JOHN/WILLIAMMR` (6:03).

3. **Phone/Contact Field (6:40-7:21):** 
   - **Mobile:** Input using `AP[CountryCode][Number]` (e.g., `AP8287...`) (6:50).
   - **Email:** Input using `API[EmailAddress]` (7:09).

4. **Ticketing/Time Limit (7:37-7:44):** 
   - Use the command `TKOK` to confirm the ticketing arrangement.

5. **Received From (7:53-8:07):** 
   - The final element to generate the PNR is `RF[Name/Source]`. Once this is entered, the system creates the unique PNR reference number (7:56).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus (GDS) Session - 5 Pricing in AMADEUS.  FXX , FXR, FXP, FXB, FXR/K, FXB/K
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/bDTeEq6hAbE
This video from *Air Travel School* provides a comprehensive guide on **Pricing Commands** in the *Amadeus* GDS system. Pricing is a critical final step in the PNR (Passenger Name Record) lifecycle to determine the cost of a ticket before finalization.

### **Core Pricing Commands**

The video explains the following command categories:

**1. Pricing in the Same Class of Service**
*   **`FX`**: Used to check the available fares in the specific class of service (e.g., Economy) already booked in the PNR. It is for information purposes only (2:22).
*   **`FXP`**: Used to save or "pick" the fare within the same class of service you have already booked (3:33).

**2. Pricing for Lowest Available Fare**
*   **`FXR`**: Used to check the lowest available fare across all classes for the flights booked. This is informative and does not save the fare (5:53).
*   **`FXB`**: Used to save the lowest available fare to the PNR. This will automatically update the PNR with the cheaper fare (7:04).

**3. Pricing with Cabin Respect**
*   **`FXR/K`**: Used to check the lowest fare within a specific cabin (e.g., Business class), respecting the cabin choice but finding the cheapest class available in that cabin (12:06).
*   **`FXB/K`**: Used to save the lowest fare found within that specific cabin (12:49).

### **Finalizing and Managing Fares**
*   **`RF` / `ER` / `IR`**: To make any fare calculation permanent, you must use the "Receive From" command string (e.g., `RF` followed by `ER`, `ER`, `IR`) to finalize and save the PNR (4:19).
*   **`TQT`**: Used to view the currently saved fare information in the PNR (4:35).
*   **`TTH`**: Used to view the **Ticket Transaction History** (or Deleted TSTs) if you have previously saved and then deleted/overwritten fare entries (7:42).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session - 6, TICKET ISSUANCE IN AMADEUS, PNR Creation in Amadeus, Pricing and issue ticket?
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/Ls3GVVB_3Wk
This video serves as a guide for **ticket issuance in the Amadeus GDS**, focusing on the four mandatory fare elements required before a ticket can be finalized. Following a successful PNR creation and pricing, these elements ensure the booking meets all airline requirements.

### **The Four Mandatory Fare Elements**
To issue a ticket, the following elements must be present in the PNR:

1.  **Form of Endorsement (FE):** Used to convey specific information to the airline (e.g., reason for a free-of-cost change). It is often automatically populated based on fare rules but can be manual for special circumstances (0:44 - 2:42).
2.  **Form of Commission (FM):** Specifies the commission for the travel agent. If set to 0%, it is not mandatory to enter manually, as the system defaults to zero (2:45 - 3:28).
3.  **Form of Payment (FP):** Defines the payment mode (e.g., Cash, Credit Card, Check, or *EasyPay*). This must be manually added to the PNR (3:29 - 4:06).
4.  **Form of Validity/Carrier (FV):** Automatically generated by the system based on the airline selected for the booking (4:07 - 4:35).

### **Key Amadeus Commands**
*   **AN:** Availability command to search for flights (e.g., `AN 08MAR LONDXB` for a flight from London to Dubai) (6:05).
*   **SS:** Sell command to book a flight (e.g., `SS1N1` to sell one seat in the lowest class) (6:49).
*   **Name/Phone/Time Limit:** Standard entries to finalize PNR details (7:00 - 7:34).
*   **FXR:** Used for lowest fare pricing; the system automatically rebooks the passenger into the necessary fare class (7:55 - 8:15).
*   **RTF:** Used to display and verify all fare elements within the PNR (8:37 - 8:50).
*   **FP:** Used to define the payment method (e.g., `FP CASH`) (9:42).
*   **TT / TTP:** The final command to issue the ticket (10:14).
*   **RTA:** Used to retrieve and verify the PNR after ticket issuance to confirm the 13-digit ticket number (10:28 - 11:18).

# Amadeus-Session 8|Uses of FXD command| Air Travel School| lowest available fare for requested sector
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/HR4OlQSVXps
This video, presented by *Air Travel School*, explains how to use specific **Amadeus GDS commands** to find the most cost-effective fares for passengers. The session focuses on helping travel agents identify the lowest available prices across various airlines for a requested route.

### **Key Amadeus Commands Explained:**

*   **FXA (Fare Display for Booked Flight):** Used when you have already booked a specific flight and cabin class. It provides the fare for that exact booking (0:24).
*   **FXR (Lowest Fare for Booked Flight):** Used to find the cheapest possible fare within the specific flight you have already selected (0:26).
*   **FXD (Lowest Available Fare for Entire Route):** This is the primary command of the session. It searches the **entire route** (e.g., London to Dubai) to find the absolute lowest fare available across all operating airlines, rather than limiting the search to a single pre-selected flight (1:22–1:32).

### **How to use FXD (Step-by-Step):**
1.  **Check Availability:** Run an availability command for your desired route (e.g., London to Dubai). The video uses the example of checking flights from *LHR* to *DXB* (0:44).
2.  **Sell a Seat:** Select and sell a seat on one of the available flights (e.g., using `SS1Y2` for an *Emirates* flight) (0:57).
3.  **Execute Command:** Enter the `FXD` command. This will display the fare for your currently held booking at the top and list other, cheaper airline recommendations below it (1:26–2:00).
4.  **Review Recommendations:** Scroll down through the results to see alternative, more budget-friendly options from other airlines (e.g., *Saudi Airlines*, *Finnair*, etc.) (2:05–2:35).

By using **FXD**, an agent avoids the time-consuming process of checking availability and fares for every airline individually, as the system automatically pulls the best market rates for that sector.

# Amadeus Session - 9 | ARNK | Arrival Unknown |How to update ARNK in PNR | Air Travel School | Saddam
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/OqLADvGZRiU
This video from *Air Travel School* explains the concept of **ARNK (Arrival Unknown/Arrival Not Known)** within the *Amadeus* GDS (Global Distribution System). ARNK is a crucial entry used when the travel method between the destination of one flight segment and the origin of the next segment is unknown or not booked through the system (0:00 - 0:42).

### **Why Use ARNK?**
If you do not update the PNR (Passenger Name Record) with an ARNK entry when there is a gap in the travel itinerary, the system may fail to calculate the **Minimum Connecting Time (MCT)** correctly, often resulting in "Invalid Multi-Airport Connection" errors (0:10 - 1:16).

### **Amadeus Command**
*   **Command:** `SIARNK`
*   **Usage:** This command inserts an "Arrival Unknown" segment into your PNR itinerary. 
*   **Example:** If a customer flies from *London* to *Mumbai* and later from *Delhi* to *London*, the system needs an ARNK entry to bridge the gap between *Mumbai* and *Delhi* (0:45 - 1:30).

### **Procedure Summary**
1.  **Identify the gap:** Recognize when a journey is missing between two booked segments (0:52 - 1:00).
2.  **Update the PNR:** Use `SIARNK` to insert the segment (1:25 - 1:30).
3.  **Verify:** Once updated, the system will recognize the itinerary structure, allowing you to check the **Minimum Connecting Time** successfully without system errors (1:41 - 1:56).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# How To Convert Zulu Time into Local Time in Amadeus?Amadeus Full Course #airlinejobs #bpojob #trick
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/r2ecesRnn4c
This video explains how to convert **Zulu time** (the standard time used in the *Amadeus* GDS system) into **local time** for specific locations. Since all timing information in *Amadeus* is initially displayed in Zulu format, this command is essential for travel professionals to understand local schedules.

### **The Command: DD**

The primary command used for this conversion is **DD** followed by specific parameters.

*   **Command Structure:** `DD[Zulu Time][Airport/City Code]`

### **Examples Provided:**

1.  **Converting to Delhi Time:**
    *   **Input:** `DD1030DEL`
    *   **Explanation:** By entering `DD` followed by the time `10:30` and the location code `DEL` (Delhi), the system calculates and displays the corresponding local time, showing it was 4:00 (0:15 - 0:32).

2.  **Converting to Dubai Time:**
    *   **Input:** `DD1530DXB`
    *   **Explanation:** Using the same command logic, entering `DD` followed by `15:30` and the code `DXB` (Dubai) results in a local time conversion of 7:30 PM (0:34 - 0:48).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session-10 GDS Training |  ATC | Automated Ticket Changer |Reissue in amadeus #gds #amadeus
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/vW0ILfxWuBo
This video provides a comprehensive guide to **reissuing or exchanging tickets** in the *Amadeus* GDS system, specifically focusing on the **Automated Ticket Changer (ATC)** process.

### **Types of Ticket Exchanges**
*   **Voluntary:** Initiated by the passenger (e.g., date change). This typically involves **penalties** and potential **fare differences** (0:52 - 2:05).
*   **Involuntary:** Initiated by the airline (e.g., flight cancellation, technical issues). Usually, no penalties or fare differences are charged (1:02 - 1:58).

### **ATC Command Process (Before Departure)**
When reissuing a ticket before any travel has occurred, you should use the automated tools first (3:20 - 3:45).

1.  **Availability:** Search for the new date and route while maintaining the same airline and the original (or higher) booking class. 
    *   *Command:* `AN [Date][Month] [Origin][Destination]/A[Airline Code]` (Example: `AN 12MAY LHRDXB/AEK` (6:25 - 7:12).
2.  **Sell New Flight:** 
    *   *Command:* `SS 1 [Class][Line Number]` (Example: `SS 1 U3`) (7:47 - 8:13).
3.  **Delete Old Segment:**
    *   *Command:* `XE [Line Number]` (8:25 - 8:36).
4.  **ATC Fare/Penalty Calculation:** 
    *   `FXQ`: Automatically calculates fare differences and penalties in the selected class (8:47 - 9:19).
    *   `FXK`: Used to find the cheapest fare in the same cabin if `FXQ` is unsuccessful (4:41 - 4:55).
    *   `TQR`: Displays the fare details clearly (9:55 - 10:28).
5.  **Payment Verification:** Check if the penalty is collected via tax codes or an EMD (Electronic Miscellaneous Document) (10:29 - 11:03).
6.  **Finalizing the Exchange:** 
    *   Check form of payment with `RTF` (11:03 - 11:29).
    *   Issue the new ticket: `TTP/TSTT[Number]/RT` (11:45 - 12:00).

### **Post-Exchange Verification**
*   **`RTF`**: View the updated PNR to see both the old and new ticket numbers (12:13 - 12:20).
*   **`TWD/T[Ticket Number]`**: View the status of the specific tickets. The old ticket status will change to **'E'** (Exchanged), and the new ticket will be shown as valid (**'O'**) (12:24 - 12:53).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session-20 |Manual Exchange in amadeus, penalty under EMD| #AirTravelSchool #gdstraining
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/hoBbq_qIrAk
This video serves as a technical tutorial on performing a **manual ticket exchange** in *Amadeus* when an airline charges a penalty through an *Electronic Miscellaneous Document (EMD)*. The process involves splitting the fare difference (collected via TST) and the penalty (collected via EMD).

### **Key Steps and Commands**

**1. Preparation & Pricing:**
* **Check Office ID:** `RHFA` and `JD` verify the office ID. (0:14)
* **Review Ticket Image:** `TWD` confirms the current ticket status. (0:19)
* **Search New Flights:** `AN 12AUG LHRDXB` finds available flights for the desired date and route. (1:01)
* **Sell New Segment:** `SS1C1` books the required class. (1:34)
* **Delete Old Segment:** `XE2` removes the previous, unused itinerary line. (1:49)
* **Calculate Fare Difference:** `FX` calculates the new fare (1:59), then `TQT` captures the fare details. (2:09)

**2. TST (Ticket Settlement Plan) Processing:**
* **Price Ticket:** `TTK` is used to create the TST. (4:42)
* **Adjust Fare:** `TTK/TGBP1560` adds the base fare difference of 1,560 GBP to the TST. (5:42)
* **Finalize TST:** `F4*` creates the pricing line (6:00), and `X9` with `FPO/CASH+/CASH` updates the form of payment. (6:24)

**3. EMD (Electronic Miscellaneous Document) for Penalty:**
* **Identify Fee Code:** `EJS/FEE/BA` lists valid reason codes for the airline (*British Airways*). (7:06)
* **Create SVC Line:** `IUBA NN1 PNF/LHR/12AUG` generates the service line for reservation change fees. (7:44)
* **Generate Mask:** `TACE/BA` creates the EMD mask. (8:29)
* **Modify/Price EMD:** `TM` with `CV100/FGBP100/CR.../FP-CASH` sets the penalty amount, currency, remarks, and payment method. (8:42)

**4. Final Issuance:**
* **Record & End:** `RFS;ER` registers the changes in the PNR. (9:27)
* **Issue Documents:** `TTP/TT4/TTM1/RT` processes the TST (ticket) and EMD simultaneously. (9:40)
* **Verification:** `RTTN` confirms that both the ticket (ET) and the EMD (DT) have been successfully issued. (10:18)

# Amadeus Session -19 |EMD (Electronic Miscellaneous Document) in Amadeus| #AirTravelSchool #gds
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/Xjj0-PK6acw
This video from *Air Travel School* explains how to issue an **Electronic Miscellaneous Document (EMD)** in *Amadeus*, debunking the common misconception that an EMD can only be created within an existing PNR. You can create a separate EMD for reasons like group deposits or penalties without a standard travel booking.

### **Process and Commands for Issuing an EMD**

1.  **Setting the Name:** Before creating the EMD, you must have a passenger or group name in the system (0:25).
    *   *Example:* `NM1SPORTS GROUP`

2.  **Finding the Reason Code:** Use the EMD reason code inquiry command to find the correct code for your specific requirement (e.g., a group deposit) (0:48).
    *   *Command:* `EGST/VKL` (Replace 'KL' with the relevant airline code) (0:54).

3.  **Initiating the EMD:** Use the inquiry command to start the EMD process, specifying the airline, journey origin, and date (1:17).
    *   *Command:* `IU KL N/DEPO/LHR/20AUG` (Where `DEPO` is the reason code, `LHR` is the origin, and `20AUG` is the date) (1:21).

4.  **Defining Financial Details:** Instead of running multiple separate commands, you can combine these into one efficient entry (3:06).
    *   *Command:* `TM/CV500/FGB500/CR-EMD ISSUED FOR GROUP DEPOSIT/FP-CASH` (1:54 - 2:15)
    *   **CV:** Coupon Value
    *   **FGB:** Face/Fare Value
    *   **CR:** Remark field
    *   **FP:** Form of Payment (e.g., Cash)

5.  **Finalizing and Issuing:** After inputting the time limit and phone contact fields, use the final command to issue the document (2:37).
    *   *Command:* `TTM/RT` (2:37)

By consolidating the financial data into a single line, travel agents can significantly speed up the issuance process for various services like group deposits or penalties.

Amadeus Session-11|ATC Exchange with No Penalty| How to reissue ticket by ATC command| #GDS.
This video provides a practical guide on how to perform an **Automated Ticket Changer (ATC)** exchange in *Amadeus* when there is **no penalty** involved in the fare rules. The session demonstrates the process of changing a flight date and reissuing a ticket for the same airline.

### Step-by-Step ATC Exchange Process:

1.  **Retrieve PNR:** Use **`RT[PNR Number]`** to open the booking (0:25).
2.  **Check New Availability:** Search for the new travel date using the standard availability command, e.g., **`AN[Date][Sector]`** (e.g., `AN12MARLHRDOH`) (0:48).
3.  **Book New Flight:** Use the sell command **`SS1[Class][Segment Number]`** (e.g., `SS1V1`). Note: You must book the same or a higher booking class (1:27, 2:13).
4.  **Cancel Old Itinerary:** Use **`XE[Segment Number]`** to remove the old flight date (e.g., `XE3`) (2:23).
5.  **Calculate Fare Difference:** Execute **`FXQ`** to retrieve the automated pricing and check for differences in base fare or taxes (2:37).
    *   **Note:** Use **`TQR`** if you need a more detailed breakdown of the fare calculation (3:10).
6.  **Verify with Customer:** Confirm the total amount to be collected (Total Add Collect) with the customer (3:39).
    *   **Abort process:** If the customer disagrees, use **`IR`** (Ignore and Retrieve) to revert to the original PNR state (3:51).
    *   **Proceed:** If they agree, save the booking (e.g., **`RF[Name]`** then **`ET`**) (4:06).
7.  **Reissue Ticket:** Locate the TST number (e.g., 5) and run **`TTP/ET[TST Number]`** (e.g., `TTP/ET5`) (4:24).
8.  **Final Verification:** Use **`RTT`** to view all ticket numbers and **`ETD[Ticket Number]`** to view the new ticket image and confirm it matches the updated itinerary (4:35, 4:48).

**Key Rule:** When reissuing, you generally must remain on the same airline. If changing airlines, prior approval is required (1:00).
वेलकम बैक टू यट अनदर सेशन ऑफ आमडीएस एक्सचेंज इन दिस सेशन वी विल लर्न इफ देर इज नो पेनल्टी एज पर फेयर रूल हाउ टू रीशू द टिकट थ्रू एटीसी कमांड एटीसी आप सबको पता है इट्स ऑटोमेटेड टिकट चेंजर यह सब कुछ करके देता है तो एटीसी में अगर कोई टिकट हमें री इशू करना है जिसमें पेनल्टी है ही नहीं तो वह कैसे दिखेगा वह हम देखेंगे इस सेशन में पीएनआर यहां पे हम रिट्रीव करेंगे आरटी और पीएनआर नंबर हमने डाला पीएनआर रिट्रीव हो गया जो कि 15 मार्च के लिए ऑलरेडी बुक्ड है 15 ऑफ मार्च को यहां पर कस्टमर लंदन से दोहा जा रहा है अब कस्टमर चाहता है कि वो 15th ऑफ मार्च के बदले में 12थ ऑफ मार्च को ट्रेवल करें तो 12थ ऑफ मार्च को ट्रेवल कराने के लिए कस्टमर को हमें यहां पे अवेलेबिलिटी चेक करनी पड़ेगी एन 12थ मार्च उसके बाद से हमें एक कमांड डालना होगा सेक्टर का लेट्स सपोज अ कस्टमर इज ट्रेवलिंग फ्रॉम एलएचआर टू दोहा तो वी विल पुट एलएचआर दोहा ए क्यू आर देखो रिइश्यू करते टाइम ना आप एयरलाइन चेंज नहीं कर सकते ठीक है सेम एयरलाइन में ही आपको रिइश्यू करना पड़ेगा अगर एयरलाइन चेंज कर रहे हो तो एयरलाइन से अप्रूवल लेना पड़ेगा और एयरलाइन अप्रूव करेगी तो ही आप दूसरे एयरलाइन में सीट सेल कर सकते हैं तो एक चीज तो क्लियर है थम रूल है ओबवियस सी बात है कि हमने जिस एयरलाइन में टिकट सेल किया है उसी में हम टिकट को रिइश्यू करेंगे तो हमने कतर में सेल किया था कतर में टिकट री इशू करेंगे रिइश्यू में हम जब नई अवेलेबिलिटी सर्च करते हैं तो उस टाइम प हमें एक चीज का खासा ध्यान रखना होता है कि सीट हम या तो सेम क्लास में सेल करेंगे या फिर हायर में करेंगे लोअर में नहीं कर सकते बेशक लोअर में सीटें खाली हो दे स्टिल यू कैन नॉट सेल अ सीट इन अ लोअर क्लास ऑफ़ सर्विस सो हियर आवर ओरिजिनल क्लास ऑफ़ सर्विस इज नेंसी सो वी आर गोइंग टू बुक अ दी न्यू डेट Amadeus Session-12. How to update Date Of Birth or SSR DOCS for infant child and adult in amadeus.
This video serves as a technical tutorial on how to update **Date of Birth (DOB)** and **SSR DOCS** (Secure Flight Passenger Data) for different passenger types—adults, children, and infants—within the *Amadeus* GDS system.

### **Key Commands and Procedures**

1. **Finding Command Help (0:16 - 0:42)**
   *   **Command:** `HE DOCS`
   *   **Usage:** Opens the help section for documentation entries. The instructor selects reference number `MS 169` and uses the move-down command five times to locate the correct template for inputting travel document information.

2. **Updating Adult Passengers (0:45 - 1:31)**
   *   **Procedure:** After retrieving the PNR (`RTN`), the user pastes the copied command template and modifies it to include:
       *   **Airline Code:** e.g., `6X`
       *   **DOB:** (e.g., `15SEP15`)
       *   **Gender:** `M` or `F`
       *   **Name:** Passenger name as per PNR.
       *   **Association:** `P1` (to link the SSR entry to passenger 1).

3. **Manual SSR DOCS Entry (1:39 - 2:27)**
   *   **Command:** `SR DOCS 6X HK1 - [DOB] - [GENDER] - - [NAME] /P[PassengerNumber]`
   *   **Example:** `SR DOCS 6X HK1 -----02MAR90-F--/P2` (for a female adult passenger).
   *   **Details:** The command requires the airline code, status (`HK1`), five hyphens, the date of birth, gender, name, and the passenger association number.

4. **Updating Infant Passengers (2:28 - 3:19)**
   *   **Command:** Similar to the adult command, but includes an indicator for infants.
   *   **Key Addition:** Use `MI` (Male Infant) or `FI` (Female Infant) to correctly categorize the infant status within the command string.
   *   **Example:** `SR DOCS 6X HK1 -----09JAN23-MI--/P2` (where the infant is associated with passenger 2).

### **Conclusion (3:20 - 3:33)**
*   After entering the commands, the user must save the PNR to ensure the updates are permanently applied. This process ensures that passenger information meets airline compliance standards for security and ticketing.

# Amadeus Session - 13. How to Retrieve PNR by The name of pax / Passenger in amadeus
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/qjfH2IZmd5w
This video (0:00 - 1:30) provides a practical tutorial on how to retrieve a **Passenger Name Record (PNR)** in the *Amadeus* GDS system when the specific PNR number is unknown.

### **Key Command for PNR Retrieval**

The primary command taught is used to search for bookings by a passenger's last name:

*   **Command:** `RT/` + `[Last Name]`
*   **Usage:** When you do not have the PNR number, you can use this command to pull up a list of all PNRs associated with that specific surname.
*   **Example:** If the passenger's last name is *Singh*, the command would be `RT/SINGH` (0:25 - 0:34).

### **Process Breakdown**
1.  **Execution:** After typing `RT/SINGH` and pressing Enter, the system displays a list of all PNRs associated with that name (0:34 - 0:43).
2.  **Navigation:** If the list is long, the user can scroll down to find the correct record (0:39 - 0:41).
3.  **Verification:** Once the list appears, the agent must ask the customer for their full name, travel date, and route to identify the correct record (0:46 - 1:00).
4.  **Final Retrieval:** Once identified (e.g., on line 39), the agent uses the command `RT` followed by the line number to open the specific booking (1:09 - 1:12).
    *   **Example:** If the booking is on line 39, the command is `RT39`.

Once the PNR is retrieved, the agent can proceed with further verification or modifications requested by the customer (1:12 - 1:19).

Amadeus Session-14 How to exchange/reissue the ticket with EMD | By ATC(Automated) #AirTravelSchool
This video provides a tutorial on how to **exchange/reissue a ticket** in *Amadeus* when the airline charges a penalty via an **EMD (Electronic Miscellaneous Document)** rather than a tax code. Below is the step-by-step process and the commands used:

### Workflow for EMD-based Reissue:

1. **Retrieve PNR:** Use `RT [PNR Number]` (0:53) to access the booking.
2. **Check Ticket Status:** Use `TWD L[Line Number]` (e.g., `TWD L6`) to view the ticket image (1:18). Ensure the status is **'O' (Open)** or **'A' (Airport Control)** (1:04).
3. **Search for New Flights:** Use `AN [Date][Route]` (e.g., `AN 02MAY LHRBRU`) (1:48).
4. **Sell New Segment:** Use `SS[Number of Pax][Class][Line Number]` (e.g., `SS1S3`) to book the preferred flight (2:56). Note: The new class must be equal to or higher than the original ticket's booking class.
5. **Temporary Delete:** Use `X[Line Number]` (e.g., `X2`) to remove the old segment temporarily (3:26).
6. **Automated Ticket Changer (ATC):** Run `FXQ` (3:53) to calculate the fare difference, tax difference, and penalty amount.
7. **Verify Details:** Run `TQR` (4:29) for a detailed view of the fare calculation.
8. **Save and Issue:** 
   * Save the booking (5:06).
   * Check the EMD mask using `TQM` (5:41).
   * Execute the reissue with `TTP/ET T[TST Number]/TTM M[EMD Number]/RT` (e.g., `TTP/ET T3/TTM M1/RT`) (6:08).
9. **Confirm Reissue:** Use `RTET A` (6:35) to verify the new ticket and the EMD status (`DT` for EMD, `ET` for ticket).

### Key Commands Summary:
* **RT [PNR]**: Retrieve booking.
* **TWD L[#]**: Display ticket image.
* **AN [Date][Route]**: Search flight availability.
* **SS[Qty][Class][Line#]**: Sell new flight segment.
* **X[Line#]**: Remove segment.
* **FXQ**: Calculate reissue fare and penalty via ATC.
* **TQR**: View fare calculation details.
* **TQM**: View EMD mask.
* **TTP/ET T#/TTM M#/RT**: Command to process the ticket and EMD reissue.

वेलकम बैक टू येट अनदर सेशन ऑफ अमेयस। दिस इज योर कोच सद्दाम। लेट्स बिगिन दी जर्नी। सो बिफोर वी बिगेन दी जर्नी आई वुड रिक्वेस्ट यू टू प्लीज लाइक एंड सब्सक्राइब आवर चैनल दैट रियली मोटिवेट अस टू मेक आवर वीडियोस ऑन रेगुलर बेसिस टू एनहांस योर नॉलेज। सो इन दिस सेशन वी विल लर्न हाउ टू रीइशू द टिकट बाय एटीसी कमांड इफ एयरलाइन इज कलेक्टिंग द पेनल्टी अंडर ईएमडी। एज यू ऑलरेडी नो दैट ईएमडी मींस इलेक्ट्रॉनिक मिसलेनियस डॉक्यूमेंट। यू नो दैट देयर आर टू वे द एयरलाइन कलेक्ट द पेनल्टी। फर्स्ट वन इज टैक्स कोड एंड सेकंड वन इज ईएमडी। अब टैक्स में एयरलाइन कैसे पेनल्टी कलेक्ट करा कराती है और कैसे आप टिकट को रीइशू करते हैं। यू ऑलरेडी नो दैट। दिस इज़ द सेकंड वे टू रीइशू द टिकट वेयर इन एयरलाइन इज़ कलेक्टिंग पेनल्टी अंडर ईएमडी। सो फर्स्ट थिंग फर्स्ट वी विल रिट्रीव अ पीएआर एंड द कमांड वुड बी आरटी एंड पीएनआर नंबर। जैसे ही हम पीएआर को रिट्रीव करते हैं हमारा दूसरा स्टेप होता है टिकट का स्टेटस जानना। आप सबको पता है कि टिकट का स्टेटस या तो ओ या तो ए में होना चाहिए। तभी आप टिकट को रीइशू कर पाएंगे। तो इसके लिए हम चलते हैं। टिकट इमेज को ओपन करेंगे और टिकट इमेज को ओपन करने का कमांड है TWD एल लाइन नंबर। और हमारा टिकट लाइन नंबर छह पे दिखाई दे रहा है। तो हम यहां पे TWD एल6 कर देंगे। जैसे ही हम TWD एल6 करते हैं, हमारा टिकट का स्टेटस यहां पे दिखाई दे रहा है। एंड दैट इज ओ ओ में है वेल एंड गुड है। दैट इज गुड टू गो गुड टू रीइशू। हम रीइशू कर सकेंगे। नाउ वी विल आस्क कस्टमर कि सर अब आप कौन से डेट पर ट्रैवल करना चाहते हैं? तो लेट्स सपोज़ कस्टमर ने बताया कि ही इज़ लुकिंग टू ट्रैवल ऑन सेकंड ऑफ मई। तो व्हाट वी विल डू वी विल रन अ कमांड ए एन सेकंड मे एंड देन वी विल ऐड एलएचआर सेक्टर बी आर यू और फिर हम यह भी जांच करेंगे कि कौन से क्लास में ओरिजिनली टिकट बुक हुई थी। तो लेट्स सपोज हमारी टिकट बुक हुई थी टेंगो क्लास में। यहां पे दिखाई दे रहा है। तो हम या तो अब टेंगो में सीट सेल करेंगे या टेंगो के ऊपर वाले क्लास में करेंगे। तो हम यहां पर चलते हैं। अब कस्टमर को हम बताएंगे यहां पे कि सर तीन फ्लाइटें ऑपरेट कर रही हैं पूरे दिन में। एक सुबह 650, एक 115 और तीसरी इवनिंग 1750 की है। कस्टमर कह रहा है इवनिंग जो 1750 वाली फ्लाइट है ना दैट इज गुड फॉर मी। दैट इज लाइक कंफर्टेबल फॉर मी। सो गो अहेड एंड बुक दी इवनिंग 17:30 फ्लाइट। अब आपको पता है कि पहले आपकी ओरिजिनल टिकट टेंगो क्लास में हुई थी। तो अब या तो टिकट आप टेंगो क्लास में बेचेंगे या उससे ऊपर वाले में बेचेंगे। उससे लोअर में आप सीटें सेल नहीं कर सकते। रीइशू का यह थंब रूल होता है। तो लेट्स अस्यूम कि टेंगो क्लास में सीटें नहीं हैं। तो हम क्या करेंगे? टेंगो के ऊपर यानी शुगर क्लास में सीटें बुक करेंगे। एंड दैट वुड बी एस1 क्योंकि एक ही पैसेंजर है एस क्योंकि एस क्लास में सीटें सेल करनी है और थ्री क्योंकि कस्टमर को जो फ्लाइट पसंद आया है वह लाइन नंबर थ्री पे है तो वी विल रन ss1 s3 एंड वी विल हिट दी एंटर बटन एज़ सून ऐज़ वी विल हिट दी एंटर बटन अ सी न्यू सी सेगमेंट विल बी सोल्ड आउट यहां पे नया सेगमेंट हमारा दिखाई देने लग गया जो कि सेकंड ऑफ़ मई का है। पुराने सेगमेंट की अभी हमें जरूरत नहीं है। तो हम इसके टेंपरेरी बेस पर डिलीट कर देंगे x2 करके। x और टू टू इसलिए क्योंकि जो सेगमेंट हमें नहीं चाहिए वो लाइन नंबर टू पे है। तो x2 करके हमने इसको टेंपरेरी बेस पे डिलीट कर दिया। परमानेंट डिलीट नहीं किया है क्योंकि अभी कस्टमर एग्री नहीं है ना कि वो वाकई में फेयर डिफरेंस और पेनल्टी पे करना चाहता है कि नहीं चाहता है। तो अभी हमने इसको टेंपरेरी बेस पे डिलीट किया है। तो हमने डिलीट कर दिया। आरटी कर लिया। वापस से पीएआर पे आ गए। कमांड रन करेंगे एफएक्सक एस सून एस यू विल रन एफएक्सक इट विल डू ईच एंड एवरीथिंग इट विल कैलकुलेट एवरीथिंग एंड इट विल गिव यू द फाइनल ग्रैंड टोटल अमाउंट तो यहां पे देखो यह कह रहा है कि ओरिजिनल बेस फेयर आपका 54 का था नया बेस फेयर आपका 62 का है बेस फेयर डिफरेंस आपका 8 पाउंड का है। ओल्ड टैक्स आपका 61 का था। नया टैक्स आपका 64 का है। तो टैक्स डिफरेंस आपका 3.40 का है। यानी टोटल जो टिकट का डिफरेंस है वह 11.40 है। पेनल्टी आपका जो है 51 पाउंड का है। ग्रैंड टोटल जो आपका बनता है वह बनता है 62 पाउंड का। अगर यह सारे इंफॉर्मेशन आपको टी क्यूआर में देखना है मतलब और भी डिटेल वे में देखना है या रिकंफर्म करना है तो आप यहां पे टी क्यू आर कर सकते हैं। कमांड होता है जिससे आप यह सारी इनेशन डिटेल वे में देख सकते हैं। तो वही इंफॉर्मेशन यहां पे भी दिखेगा। कुछ अलग नहीं दिखेगा बट थोड़ा डिटेल वे में दिखेगा। तो यह देखो यह सब कुछ फिर से वही चीज दिखाई दे रहा है। बेस फेयर डिफरेंस, टैक्स डिफरेंस उसके बाद से पेनल्टी अमाउंट और ग्रैंड टोटल। कस्टमर को आपने बताया कि कस्टमर सर और मैम और मिस्टर अहमद यू विल हैव टू पे 64 62.40 आर यू रेडी टू पे दिस? कस्टमर ने बोला हां हां आई एम रेडी टू पे इट। तो आप यहां पे जाएंगे और टिकट को सेव कर देंगे। जैसे आप टिकट को सेव करेंगे। अब यह टिकट रीइशू होने के लिए तैयार है। तो आप टीटी करके देखेंगे। अरे यहां पे तो 62 होना चाहिए। 11.40 क्यों है? तो गाइस मैंने जैसा कि आपको बताया था स्टार्टिंग में ही कि इसमें पेनल्टी ईएमडी में जाएगी। यानी जो टीएसटी में है सिर्फ बेस पेयर और टैक्सेस का डिफरेंस है। जो पेनल्टी अमाउंट है उसके लिए एक सेपरेट मास्क बना हुआ है जिसे हम ईएमडी मास्क कहते हैं। जैसे आप टी क्यूएम करोगे आपको आपका ईएमडी मास्क दिखाई दे जाएगा जो कि 51 का है। 51 + 11.40 करोगे आप तो यह 62.40 बनेगा जो कि हमारा ग्रैंड टोटल था। तो दिस इज़ हाउ वी रीइशू द टिकट इफ एयरलाइन इज़ कले कलेक्टिंग द पेनल्टी अंडर ईएमडी। अब आपको यहां पे कुछ नहीं करना है। बिल्कुल वही कमांड चलाएंगे टीटीp वाला। बस इसमें थोड़ा सा एक एडिशनल कमांड हो जाएगा जिसमें आप टीएसएम को भी इशू करेंगे यानी ईएमडी को भी इशू करेंगे। तो टीएसटी नंबर हमारा जो है वो है थ्री। तो हम यहां पे कमांड रन करेंगे ttp ईटी t3 ttm यह कमांड इंक्रीज हो जाएगा एम और अपना ईएमडी नंबर डाल दो। यहां पे मेरा ईएमडी नंबर है वन। तो मैं यहां पे वन डाल दूंगा और स्लैश आरटी कर देंगे। एज सून एज यू विल रन दिस कमांड, योर टिकट विल बी रीइशूड। मैंने एंटर कर दिया। टिकट हमारा रीइशू हो गया। अब हम करेंगे यहां पे आरटीईटीए टू चेक वेदर टिकट हैज़ बीन रीइशूड ऑ नॉट। जैसे हम आरटीईटीए करेंगे। यहां पे देखो दो टिकटें दिखाई देने लग गई। एक लाइन नंबर सात पे और एक लाइन नंबर नौ पे। आप कहोगे कि आठ पे भी तो दिखाई दे रही है। आठ पे टिकट नंबर नहीं है। यह ईएमडी नंबर है। मुझे कैसे पता चला कि यह ईएमडी नंबर है? यहां पे देखो डीटी लिखा हुआ है और यहां पे ईटी लिखा हुआ है। ईटी का मतलब होता है टिकट और डीटी का मतलब होता है ईएमडी नंबर। ठीक है? तो हमारा सात और नौ पे टिकट नंबर दिखाई दे रहा है। हम यहां पे कमांड रहेंगे TWD एल7 और फिर इसको स्क्रीन को डिवाइड करके यहां पे कमांड रन करेंगे TWD ओ स्टार तो हमारा ओरिजिनल टिकट खुल जाएगा। यह देखो हमारा ओरिजिनल टिकट ई मोड में जा चुका है। नया टिकट ओपन मोड में है। दिस इज़ हाउ वी कैन रीइशू द टिकट बाय कलेक्टिंग द पेनल्टी अंडर ईएमडी बाय एटीसी कमांड। थैंक यू सो मच फॉर वाचिंग दिस। प्लीज डू लाइक एंड सब्सक्राइब टू मोटिवेट अस एंड टू मेक अ वीडियोस ऑन रेगुलर बेसिस। थैंक यू सो मच फॉर वाचिंग। बाय-ब।
# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session 15 | HOW TO CHECK FARE RULE IN AMADEUS | FQD |#AirTravelSchool #gdstraining #amadeus
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/Us035xRodoo
This video from *Air Travel School* explains how to check **fare rules** within the *Amadeus* GDS (Global Distribution System). Checking these rules is critical before committing to any changes, cancellations, or reissues for a customer. 

### **Process Overview**
1. **Open Ticket Image:** Retrieve the ticket details to extract necessary data such as the sector, airline, class of service, departure date, and issuance date.
2. **Check Fare Rules:** Use the specific *Amadeus* command to view the rules associated with the fare.
3. **Identify Fare Basis:** Locate the specific fare basis code within the results to understand conditions like non-refundability or change fees.

### **Key Amadeus Commands**

*   **Display Ticket Image:** `TWD/LN` (where LN is the line number of the ticket).
    *   *Example:* `TWD/5` to open the ticket at line number 5 (1:16).
*   **Fare Rules Display (FQD):** `FQDsector/Aairline code/Cclass of service/Ddeparture date/R,UP,issuance date`
    *   *Breakdown:* 
        *   `FQD`: The base command for fare display.
        *   `LHRDXB`: The travel sector (e.g., London Heathrow to Dubai).
        *   `/A`: Airline code (e.g., `/ABA` for British Airways).
        *   `/C`: Class of service (e.g., `/CV` for Victor class).
        *   `/D`: Departure date (e.g., `/D15APR24`).
        *   `/R,UP,`: Mandatory command parameters for issuance date.
    *   *Example:* `FQD LHRDXB/ABA/CV/D15APR24/R,UP,29JAN24` (2:08 - 2:57).
*   **Specific Fare Rule Retrieval:** Once the fare basis is identified in the list, use the command `FQN30/P` (where 30 is the line number of the specific fare basis found) to open the detailed rules (4:25).

### **Tips for Efficiency**
*   **Searching Lists:** When `FQD` returns multiple pages of results, use `Ctrl+F` to search for your specific fare basis code to save time instead of scrolling through every page (3:47).
*   **Interpreting Rules:** Once the rules are open, you can scroll down to find sections regarding **Changes** or **Cancellations** to inform the customer about fees (e.g., "GBP 150 for reissue") or refund eligibility (4:51 - 5:01).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session 16 |Manual Exchange| How to Exchange/Reissue if Airline Collects Penalty in TAX Code
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/rAw6yvXCH3I
This video serves as a technical tutorial on performing a **manual ticket exchange/reissue** in *Amadeus* when the *Automated Ticket Changer (ATC)* fails. Unlike automated processes, manual exchange requires verifying fare rules, calculating fare differences, and processing penalty fees separately.

### **Step-by-Step Manual Exchange Process & Commands**

1. **PNR Retrieval:**
   * Command: `RT [PNR Number]` (1:38)
   * Use: Retrieves the booking from the system.

2. **Ticket Status Check:**
   * Command: `TWD [Line Number]` (2:08)
   * Use: Verifies if the ticket is in 'O' (Open) status, which is required for a reissue.

3. **Fare Rule Analysis:**
   * Command: `FQ D[Sector]/D[Date]R,UP` (3:41)
   * Use: Checks fare conditions to find the penalty charge.
   * Command: `FQA[Line Number]*P` (4:53)
   * Use: Opens the specific fare rule details to identify the change fee (e.g., 150 GBP).

4. **Price Comparison:**
   * Command: `WP` (or pricing command for new fare) (7:09)
   * Command: `TWD` and `TWD/TAX` (7:45)
   * Use: Calculates the difference between the new fare and the original ticket fare (including tax breakdown).

5. **Updating Pricing & Taxes:**
   * Command: `TQT` (10:11) - Initiates pricing.
   * Command: `TTK/X` (10:57) - Updates the penalty in a specific tax code (e.g., `XP` for *British Airways*).
   * Command: `TTK/[Penalty Code]/T[Currency][Total Amount]` (11:07) - Adds the total calculated reissue amount.

6. **Issuing the Reissue:**
   * Command: `FO[Original Ticket Line Number]` (11:43)
   * Use: Creates the link between the old and new ticket.
   * Command: `FPO/[Payment Method]+/[Payment Method]` (12:05)
   * Use: Defines the form of payment (e.g., cash).
   * Command: `TTP/TT[TST Number]/RT` (12:33)
   * Use: Finalizes the issuance of the new ticket.

7. **Verification:**
   * Command: `RTTN` (12:48)

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session-17 Manual TST| How to create a Manual Mask/TST in Amadeus| Saddam| #AirTravelSchool
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/53zmN39-Wa8
This video by *Air Travel School* provides a detailed tutorial on how to create a **Manual TST (Transitional Stored Ticket)** in *Amadeus*. A manual TST is necessary in scenarios such as when a fare has expired, when dealing with group bookings, or when an airline provides a specific waiver to issue a ticket at a previous fare (0:00 - 1:53).

### **Step-by-Step Manual TST Commands**

The instructor outlines the following command sequence to manually build the ticket mask:

*   **Generate TST:** Use `TTC` to create a new, empty TST mask (2:04).
*   **Fare Basis Code:** Enter the code provided by the airline using `TTK/B[FareBasisCode]` (2:20).
*   **Validity Dates:** Set the 'Not Valid Before/After' dates using `TTK/V[Date]` (2:40).
*   **Baggage Allowance:** Specify the baggage limit (e.g., 2PC) using `TTK/A[BaggageInfo]` (2:52).
*   **Base Fare:** Input the base fare amount and currency using `F[Currency][Amount]` (3:06).
*   **Taxes:** Apply tax information using `TTK/X[TaxDetails]` (3:27).
*   **Fare Calculation Line:** Define the routing logic using `TTK/C[Routing]` (3:47).
*   **Tour Code & Waiver:** Enter the waiver or tour code into the TST using `TT*[Code]` (4:10).
*   **Endorsement Line:** Add the required endorsement line provided by the airline (4:37).
*   **Form of Payment:** Specify the payment method (e.g., `FP CASH`) to finalize the ticket (4:47).

### **Finalizing and Verification**
Once the fields are updated, the user saves the entry and executes `TTP/ET` followed by `RT` to issue the ticket (4:58). The instructor demonstrates how to verify the TST by checking for the **"m+1"** indicator, which confirms the TST is manually created rather than automated (5:09 - 5:40).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session-18 |Manual Exchange in Amadeus| Penalty in TAX | #AirTravelSchool #gdstraining
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/M6y3YvZN8F4
This video (Amadeus Session-18) provides a comprehensive guide on performing **Manual Ticket Exchanges** in *Amadeus* when an airline charges a penalty via a tax code and there are variations in base fares and taxes. 

### Core Steps for Manual Exchange:

1.  **Preparation & Verification:**
    *   **Verify Office ID:** Use `RHF` and `JD` to ensure you are working within the correct office environment (0:41).
    *   **Open Ticket Image:** `TWD/LN` (e.g., `TWD/A6`) accesses the electronic ticket image (0:51).
    *   **Check Fare Rules:** Use `FQD` followed by the sector, airline code, class, and date (1:13) to view fare rules. Specifically, use `FQA` + line number + `/16` to view penalty category 16 (1:59).
    *   **Currency Conversion:** If penalties are in a different currency, use `FQC` (e.g., `FQC60EUR/GBP`) to convert to the local currency (4:01).

2.  **Booking New Itinerary:**
    *   **Availability:** Search for new flight availability using `AN` + date + route + airline code (5:34).
    *   **Sell Seats:** Use `SS` (e.g., `SS1H3`) to book new flights (6:33). Old segments are removed using `XI` (6:44).
    *   **Price New Fare:** Use `FXP` to generate a new pricing record (6:52).

3.  **Fare & Tax Calculation:**
    *   **Retrieve Old Data:** Access the old ticket via `TW` and `TWD/TAX` to see the original tax breakdown (7:25).
    *   **Calculate Differences:** Calculate the difference between the new and old base fares and taxes using `DF` (e.g., `DF187-59`) (8:17).

4.  **TST (Transitional Stored Ticket) Manipulation:**
    *   **Prepare TST:** Use `TTK/C` to move taxes to a status where they can be modified (9:50).
    *   **Clean Taxes:** Use `TTK/O` to remove incorrect data and `TTK/O` again to re-add original taxes from the ticket image (10:20).
    *   **Add New Taxes/Penalty:** Use `TTK/X` to add the calculated tax difference and penalty code (11:07).

5.  **Finalizing & Reissuing:**
    *   **Finalize TST:** Ensure the total payable amount matches the expected figure (11:23).
    *   **Update Form of Payment:** Use `TTP/FPO/CASH` to set payment type (11:58).
    *   **Save & Reissue:** Save changes with `TQT`, and attempt reissue with `TTP` (12:15). If a 'Need NV' error occurs, use `TTK/NV` to update the 'Not Valid Before/After' dates (12:35).
    *   **Verification:** Use `RT` and `TWD/A...` to confirm the ticket is in 'E' (Exchange) mode (13:16).

aHow to convert Currency in amadeus? #airlinejobs #bpojob #travelindustry #education #amadeus #gds

This video serves as a tutorial on how to perform **currency conversion** within the *Amadeus* global distribution system. The speaker demonstrates specific commands used to calculate exchange rates for various currencies.

### **Amadeus Currency Conversion Command**

The fundamental command used for converting currencies in *Amadeus* is **`FQC`** (Fare Quote Currency).

**Command Structure:** `FQC[Amount][Source Currency][Target Currency]`

### **Examples Provided in the Video:**

1.  **US Dollars to Indian Rupees:**
    *   **Command:** `FQC500USD/INR` (0:16)
    *   **Use:** This command instructs the system to convert 500 *US Dollars* into *Indian Rupees*.
    *   **Result:** The system provides an approximate conversion, showing that 500 *USD* equals approximately 41,000 *INR* (0:21).

2.  **US Dollars to Hong Kong Dollars:**
    *   **Command:** `FQC500USD/HKD` (0:32)
    *   **Use:** This command instructs the system to convert 500 *US Dollars* into *Hong Kong Dollars*.
    *   **Result:** The system displays the conversion, indicating that 500 *USD* is worth approximately 3,920 *HKD* (0:37).

# How To Check Minimum Connecting Time in Amadeus? #gds #amadeus #bpojob #galileo #airlinejobs
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/MstdE6uiPuU
This video explains the importance of **Minimum Connecting Time (MCT)** in *Amadeus* when booking connecting flights to ensure passengers can successfully board their next flight. If the MCT is not met, the passenger will not be able to catch the next flight (0:00-0:17).

Here are the *Amadeus* commands covered in the video:

*   **DMI (Display Minimum Connecting Time - Entire PNR):**
    *   **Use:** Checks the MCT for all flights or segments within an entire *PNR* (Passenger Name Record).
    *   **Example:** Typing `DMI` provides a comprehensive view of the connecting time requirements for the whole itinerary (0:18-0:28).

*   **DM (Display Minimum Connecting Time - Segment Specific):**
    *   **Use:** Checks the MCT for a specific segment pair.
    *   **Command Format:** `DM` + `segment number` (e.g., `DM1/2`).
    *   **Example:** By using `DM1/2`, the system displays the required minimum time versus the actual connecting time. In the video example, the requirement is 1 hour, and the actual connection is 1 hour and 10 minutes, confirming the connection is valid (0:29-0:52).

# Amadeus Session 21 Name Correction & Name Change in Amadeus #gds #jobs #travel
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/D0vjp4vjS-E
This video (0:00 - 4:46) explains the process of **correcting or changing names** for bookings in the *Amadeus* GDS system. The narrator emphasizes that any name modification is subject to **airline approval**.

### Key Prerequisites
* **Airline Approval:** You must obtain permission from the airline before attempting a change.
* **De-linking:** If a ticket has already been issued, the airline must first 'de-link' it to grant you the rights to amend the name.

### Commands and Usage

#### 1. Changing First and Last Name
To modify both the first and last name for a passenger (e.g., located on line 1 of the PNR), use the following syntax (1:07 - 2:02):
* **Command:** `A1` followed by the new last name, a slash, and the new first name.
* **Example:** `A1HUSSEIN/SADDAM MR` (Updates the name of the passenger at line 1 to *Saddam Hussein*).
* **Action:** After entering the command, a pop-up will appear requiring you to confirm that you are adhering to airline rules.

#### 2. Changing Only the First Name
If you only need to update the first name or correct a spelling error while keeping the last name intact, you can use a simpler command (2:07 - 2:47):
* **Command:** Passenger line number followed by a slash and the new name.
* **Example:** `1/SADDAM MR` (Updates the first name for passenger at line 1).

#### 3. Finalizing Changes
Once the name is amended, you must finalize the update in the system (2:52 - 2:58):
* **Command:** `RFSE` (Receive From / Sign-off) and `RIR` (Redisplay PNR) to save and refresh the record.

### Important Considerations
* **Group Bookings:** While some airlines allow full name changes, especially in group bookings, these are highly restricted. Attempting to change a name without prior approval will result in an error message from the system, such as *"Restricted - Call Airline"* (4:15 - 4:26).
* **System Flexibility:** Always verify the specific rules of the carrier you are working with, as policies regarding name corrections vary significantly between airlines.

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session 22 _ Amadeus All Types of Refunds_ #gdstraining #amadeus  #jobs #travelagent
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/3Lhtsi8FuZ4
This video provides a comprehensive guide on handling various types of **airline ticket refunds** within the *Amadeus* GDS system. The tutorial covers voluntary, involuntary, and partial refunds, detailing the specific manual commands required for each scenario.

### Core Refund Commands
* **TRF:** Opens the refund panel for a specific ticket.
* **TRFP:** The final command to process/claim the refund.
* **TRF/IG:** Used to ignore/cancel a refund session if needed.

### Refund Scenarios

**1. Voluntary Refunds (with Penalties)**
When a passenger initiates a refund and a penalty applies, you must deduct the penalty before finalizing:
* **Fixed Amount Penalty:** Use `TRF[TicketNumber]` followed by `TRFUCP[Amount]A` (where 'A' stands for amount) to deduct the penalty (1:23).
* **Percentage-based Penalty:** Use `TRFUCP[Percentage]P` (where 'P' stands for percentage) to calculate and deduct the penalty from the base fare (2:30).

**2. Involuntary Refunds (Full Refunds)**
For involuntary refunds (often due to airline changes), penalties are waived, but specific codes are required:
* **Waiver Code:** Enter `TAW/[Code]` to input the airline-provided waiver (3:56).
* **Refund Remark:** Use `TAW/RM[RemarkText]` to add necessary context (4:10).

**3. Partial Refunds**
This occurs when part of the journey has already been flown. The process involves:
* **Calculating Used Fare:** Use `FQPDXBDXB/A[Class]/D[Date]/[Destination]/R,UP,D[IssueDate]` to determine the cost of the flown segment (5:20).
* **Adjusting Taxes:** Use `TRFAU/U[Amount]` to input the used amount, then `TRFAU/T` to manage tax breakdowns, deleting unused taxes and adding the remaining refundable balance (8:33 - 9:45).
* **Finalizing:** Similar to other types, execute `TRFP` to complete the transaction (10:02).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# LIVE: Amadeus Session - 23 How to Read Itinerary History in Amadeus? Find No-Show Status
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/XkEc-XhhVM0
This session of *Air Travel School* focuses on reading and interpreting the **Itinerary History** in *Amadeus*, which is essential for understanding booking modifications, flight status changes, and identifying "no-show" passengers.

### **Key Commands Explained**

*   **RHA or RHI:** These are the primary commands to display the full **Itinerary History** (0:46). This allows you to track every change made to the PNR since its creation.
*   **DD[Date] / [City Code]:** A useful command to convert **Zulu time** (UTC) to the **local time** of a specific city. For example, `DDT 4SEP / DEL` allows you to see what the local time in *Delhi* was when an action was performed (7:42 - 8:53).
*   **DACN[Code]:** Used to decode city or location codes (e.g., `DACNSPL` to identify an airport or office location) (12:51).

### **Understanding Status Codes**

*   **Segment Status:** Indicates the lifecycle of specific flight segments within the booking.
    *   **OS:** Original Segment (3:09)
    *   **AS:** Added Segment (3:11)
    *   **XS:** Cancelled Segment (3:15)
    *   **TC:** Time Change (3:17)
    *   **DL:** Deleted Segment (3:21)
    *   **CS:** Confirmed/Changed Segment (3:24)

*   **Itinerary Status:** Defines the booking's current confirmation state.
    *   **HK:** Holding Confirmed (3:52)
    *   **UN:** Un-operational flight (3:57)
    *   **TK:** Time Change (4:00)
    *   **KK:** Confirmed by airline (4:25)

### **Itinerary History Analysis**

The video demonstrates how to interpret the **Reference Line**, which provides audit details for every transaction, including:
*   **Office ID and IATA number:** Identifies where the booking was created (6:12 - 6:51).
*   **Sign-in IDs:** Shows which agent (e.g., *S. Kumar*) made the changes (5:43).
*   **Timestamp analysis:** By cross-referencing the Zulu time of an action with local time, agents can determine exactly when a modification occurred, helping resolve disputes or verify no-show penalties (2:42, 7:12).

An "XS" status usually indicates a cancellation by the travel agent, while a "UN" status triggered by a system-generated change often implies an airline schedule change requiring agent intervention (13:12 - 13:42).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# IATA & Traffic Conference Areas (TC Area) #gdstraining  #AirTravelSchool #amadeus #gds #jobs
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/pblZT91cbHI
This video serves as an introductory lecture on the **International Air Transport Association (IATA)** and the **Traffic Conference (TC) Areas** of the world, which are foundational concepts for anyone entering the airline and travel industry.

### **Overview of IATA**
Established in 1945 in *Havana, Cuba*, **IATA** (International Air Transport Association) is a global trade organization for airlines (0:32 - 0:47). Its key responsibilities include:
* **Formulating industry policies and standards** (1:10).
* **Setting tariffs and facilitating price-fixing** (1:14 - 1:23).
* Supporting airline activities to ensure safety and efficiency.
* Currently, IATA boasts over 290 member airlines across 128 countries, with its headquarters located in *Montreal, Canada* (1:26 - 1:33).

### **IATA Traffic Conference (TC) Areas**
To standardize travel routes and fare calculations, IATA divides the world into three specific **TC Areas** (1:42 - 1:53):

* **TC Area 1 (Western Hemisphere):** Includes *North America*, *South America*, and the surrounding regions (2:05 - 2:08).
* **TC Area 2 (Eastern Hemisphere):** Comprises *Europe*, the *Middle East*, and *Africa* (2:10 - 2:14).
* **TC Area 3 (Eastern Hemisphere):** Covers *Asia*, *Russia*, and *Australia* (2:18 - 2:20).

The video also provides a breakdown of the world's seven major continents: *Asia*, *Africa*, *North America*, *South America*, *Antarctica*, *Europe*, and *Australia* (3:42 - 3:54).

### **Note on Commands**
While the video title and description mention *Amadeus GDS* commands (such as pricing, fare checking, and class availability), these specific technical commands were **not demonstrated** in this particular video segment. The content focuses entirely on the conceptual and geographical foundations of the airline industry.

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Session-24. Travel Terminology Part 1: No-Show, One-Way, Direct Flights & More! #AirTravelSchool
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/B0fH7FRyE-U
This session from *Air Travel School* provides a comprehensive overview of essential travel industry terminology and concepts required for working with Global Distribution Systems (GDS) like *Amadeus*.

### **Travel Fundamentals & Flight Types**
*   **Domestic vs. International:** Domestic travel occurs within a country (requiring a ticket and ID), while international travel involves crossing borders (requiring a ticket, passport, and visa) (0:42-1:52).
*   **Scheduled vs. Charter:** Scheduled flights operate on fixed, pre-determined timetables. Charter flights are rented/purchased in their entirety, allowing for custom arrival/departure schedules (1:58-3:06).

### **Flight Routing Terminology**
*   **Non-Stop:** A flight from origin to destination without any stops (4:19).
*   **Direct Flight:** A flight that may take a technical stop or layover at an intermediate point, but the passenger remains on the same plane (3:38-5:03).
*   **Connecting Flight:** A journey where the passenger must change aircraft at an intermediate point (3:15-5:38).

### **Itinerary Types**
*   **One-Way:** Travel to a destination without returning on the same ticket (5:59-6:24).
*   **Return/Round Trip:** Traveling to a destination and returning to the origin on the same ticket (6:28-6:55).
*   **Open Jaw:** A trip where the arrival and departure points are different. 
    *   **Single Open Jaw:** A surface break at one end (e.g., fly to *Dubai*, travel by road to *Abu Dhabi*, then fly back from *Abu Dhabi*) (7:28-8:22).
    *   **Double Open Jaw:** Surface points at both ends (e.g., fly from *Goa* to *Dubai*, and return from *Abu Dhabi* to *Mopa*) (8:25-9:53).
*   **Circle Trip:** Covering three or more destinations before returning to the origin (10:00-11:00).
*   **Round the World:** A journey covering all three travel zones or seven continents (11:07-11:58).

### **Passenger Type Codes (PTC)**
*   **Adult:** Age 12+.
*   **Child:** Age 2–12, traveling with a guardian.
*   **Infant:** Age 0–2 (I-N-F).
*   **UNMR (Unaccompanied Minor):** A child traveling alone (12:02-13:28).
*   **INS (Infant with Seat):** Rare; infants usually travel as INF (without a seat) associated with an adult (13:30-14:15).
*   **Titles:** Use Mister/Miss/Mrs for adults, and Master/Miss for children/infants (14:30-15:10).

### **Critical Travel Statuses**
*   **No-Show:** When a passenger with a confirmed reservation does not arrive for the flight and fails to cancel beforehand. This is a critical term for travel agents to manage (15:13-16:31).
*   **Go-Show:** A passenger without a reservation who presents themselves at the airport on a stand-by basis, hoping for an available seat if someone else no-shows (16:33-18:13).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Session-25. Travel Terminology Part-2 SSR, OSI, Code Share, ADM, ACM, BSP & ARC #airtravelschool
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/ySExOfLYgyk
This video serves as a comprehensive guide to essential **travel and airline industry terminology**, specifically tailored for GDS (Global Distribution System) users, with a focus on *Amadeus*. Here is a detailed breakdown of the concepts covered:

### **Financial and Documentation Documents**
*   **EMD (Electronic Miscellaneous Document):** An electronic receipt/voucher used worldwide (outside the US) for services not covered by a standard flight ticket, such as seat upgrades, excess baggage, or penalty payments (0:13-1:19).
*   **MCO (Miscellaneous Charges Order):** Primarily used in the *US market*, it functions like an EMD but is more advanced, allowing for partial ticket payments or managing refunds (1:22-3:27).
*   **ADM (Agency Debit Memo) & ACM (Agency Credit Memo):** These represent adjustments to agent accounts. An **ADM** is issued by an airline to recover funds when an agent violates policy or makes payment errors (often including a service charge), while an **ACM** is a credit issued to the agent, such as for overpayment or involuntary service downgrades (13:16-19:39).
*   **Void:** The process of cancelling a transaction within the same day of issuance to receive a full refund without penalties. It is sometimes misused to create "dummy tickets" for visa requirements (27:40-29:48).

### **Airline Partnerships and Logistics**
*   **Code Share:** An agreement where one airline operates a flight, and another markets it under its own flight number (3:30-4:31).
*   **Interline Agreement:** An agreement between two or more airlines allowing them to handle passengers on a single itinerary, ensuring baggage and fare policies are honored across different carriers (4:31-7:40).
*   **Layover vs. Stopover:** For international connections, a **layover** is a stop of less than 24 hours, while a **stopover** is a stay exceeding 24 hours (7:40-9:45).
*   **Schedule Change:** Modifications to flights by airlines. **Minor changes** (small time shifts) are usually accepted via the command `ERK`, while **major changes** (significant delays, cabin downgrades, or route changes) significantly impact the passenger (19:42-22:53).

### **Service Requests and Payments**
*   **SSR (Special Service Request) & OSI (Other Service Information):** Commands to inform airlines of passenger needs. **SSR** requires airline approval (e.g., special meals, wheelchairs), while **OSI** is for information that requires no approval (e.g., informing the crew that a passenger is a doctor). Both can be viewed using the command `RTG` (13:07-13:11).
*   **Form of Payment (FOP):** Specific commands in *Amadeus* are used to process card payments, including American Express (`AEX`), Visa (`VI`), and Mastercard (`CA`). The **Easy Pay** (`EP`) card, launched by IATA, functions like a pre-funded debit card (24:55-27:37).

### **Industry Systems**
*   **BSP (Billing and Settlement Plan) & ARC (Airline Reporting Corporation):** These are the central systems for ticketing data and financial settlement. **BSP** covers the world outside the US, while **ARC** is specific to the *US market* (29:48-31:49).

# Amadeus Session- 26 Difference between FXB and FXD. MASTER PRICER IN AMADEUS. #airtravelschool
This video provides a practical guide to **Amadeus GDS** (Global Distribution System) pricing commands, specifically focusing on the differences between **FXB** and **FXD**. 

### Core Commands Overview:

*   **FXB (Father X-ray Bravo):** This command is used to retrieve the lowest available fare for the **specific flight(s) you have already booked** in your PNR. It calculates the pricing based strictly on your current itinerary selection (2:07-2:25).
*   **FXD (Father X-ray Delta):** This is a powerful pricing command that displays the lowest available fare for the route while also listing **all other flight options** operating on that same route across different airlines (3:22-3:35). It is ideal when a client is price-sensitive and open to alternatives beyond the initially selected flight.

### Other Related Commands:

*   **FXZ (Number):** Used to book a specific flight option displayed in the pricing list (e.g., `FXZ3` to book the third option) (4:30-4:35).
*   **FXU (Number):** Used to book the flight and simultaneously generate the **TST (Ticket Selection/Transaction)** for the itinerary (4:38-4:48).

### Key Takeaways:

*   **Efficiency:** Using `FXD` saves significant time compared to checking every airline's fare manually, as it presents a competitive list of options ranging from lowest to highest price (3:15-3:22).
*   **Decision Making:** While `FXB` is best for confirming price on a specific, chosen flight, `FXD` acts as a consultative tool to help travel agents offer the best possible deal to customers who prioritize cost savings over specific airlines or flight times (5:39-6:07).
This video serves as a technical tutorial on **Amadeus GDS** pricing commands, specifically explaining the functional differences between `FXB` and `FXD` for travel agents. Below is a detailed summary and the requested transcript.

### Pricing Commands Overview

*   **FXB (Father X-ray Bravo):** Retrieves the lowest available fare **strictly for the flight already booked** in the PNR (2:07-2:25). Use this when you have a specific flight and just need to price it.
*   **FXD (Father X-ray Delta):** Displays the lowest available fare for the route while also providing a **comprehensive list of other flight options** across various airlines (3:22-3:35). This is essential for price-sensitive clients who are flexible with airlines.
*   **FXZ (Number):** A command used to book a specific flight option from the `FXD` list (e.g., `FXZ3` books the third option) (4:30-4:35).
*   **FXU (Number):** Used to book the flight and simultaneously generate the **TST (Ticket Selection/Transaction)** for the itinerary (4:38-4:48).

# Amadeus Session-27 How to void the ticket in amadeus? coammdn | steps | Charges & Void Window
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/pSkNwZrWcqY
This video provides a comprehensive guide on the **VOID process** in the *Amadeus GDS*, explaining how to cancel a ticket on the same day it was issued to receive a full refund without penalty (1:34). 

### **Key Concepts**
* **What is Voiding:** It cancels a ticket as if it were never issued. It is typically free, unless specific market rules (like in India or Thailand) apply (1:34 - 3:31).
* **Void Windows:** 
    * **Global:** Same day only, until 11:59 PM (3:56 - 4:48).
    * **Canada:** Within 24 hours of issuance (4:07 - 5:13).
    * **USA:** Until the next working day (5:29 - 6:23).

### **Amadeus Void Commands**

There are two primary methods to void a ticket in *Amadeus*:

**1. Voiding by Line Number**
* **Command:** `TRDC L[Line Number]`
* **Use:** Used when the ticket is already retrieved in your PNR (7:11).
* **Example:** To void a ticket located at line 8 in your PNR, use **`TRDC L8`** (8:06 - 8:22).

**2. Voiding by Ticket Number**
* **Command:** `TRDC/TK[10-digit ticket number]`
* **Use:** Used when you prefer to specify the full 10-digit ticket number (6:52).
* **Example:** To void a specific ticket, use **`TRDC/TK5578...`** (10:44 - 10:53).

### **Verification Commands**
* **Retrieve Ticket Image:** Use `TWD L[Line Number]` to open and check the status of your ticket before and after voiding (7:41).
* **Verification:** After executing the void command, the ticket status will change to **'V'** (Voided) in the ticket image (11:04 - 11:12).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session 7. How to split PNR. SPLIT OFF. How to divide passengers in amadeus. SP Command.
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/3hGbfojO2u4
This video from *Air Travel School* explains the process of **splitting a PNR** (Passenger Name Record) in the *Amadeus* GDS. Splitting is necessary when multiple passengers share one PNR, but one or more passengers need to have their travel plans changed or canceled independently without affecting the others.

### **Why Split a PNR?**
If you attempt to cancel a flight (using the `XI` command) for a single passenger in a shared PNR without splitting first, the action will apply to all passengers in that booking. Splitting creates a separate PNR for the specified passenger(s), allowing for independent modifications (1:41 - 1:57).

### **Amadeus Commands for Splitting PNR**

| Command | Use Case | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **SP[number]** | Splitting the PNR | Used to divide a specific passenger from the original group. Example: `SP2` separates the second passenger (2:02 - 2:09). |
| **RF / EF** | Confirming changes | Required to save the split process. `RF` (Receive From) confirms the request, and `EF` (End and Retrieve) finalizes the action (2:21 - 2:28). |
| **RF / ER / IR** | Permanent Split | Finalizes the permanent separation of the PNRs. `ER` (End and Retrieve) is used to save the modification (2:52 - 2:56). |
| **RT AXR** | Retrieve new PNR | Used to find the new PNR code generated for the split passenger once the process is complete (3:13 - 3:19). |
| **XI** | Cancellation | Used to cancel the flight segment for the passenger in their newly separated PNR (3:27 - 3:30). |

### **Step-by-Step Workflow**
1. **Identify the passenger:** Note the passenger number (e.g., 2) (2:05).
2. **Run the SP command:** Use `SP[number]` to initiate the split (2:09).
3. **Confirm with RF/EF:** Enter the 'Received From' data and end the session to generate the new PNR file (2:21 - 2:28).
4. **Finalize:** Use `RF`, `ER`, and `IR` to ensure the split is permanent (2:52 - 2:56).
5. **Retrieve & Act:** Use `RT AXR` to locate the new PNR for the separated passenger, then proceed with the necessary action, such as canceling the flight with `XI` (3:13 - 3:30).

# tactiq.io free youtube transcript
# Amadeus Session 28 | Involuntary Exchange Explained | FXI | Way to update waiver Code | ATS
# https://www.youtube.com/watch/aB37GpzAZm4
This video explains the process of an **Involuntary Exchange** in the *Amadeus* GDS, which occurs when an airline initiates changes to a passenger's itinerary (e.g., flight cancellations, weather issues, or strikes) and the ticket needs to be reissued without penalty or fare difference. 

### **Key Process and Commands**

1. **Check Flight Availability (2:27):** Ensure the new flight segments are confirmed in the booking. If segments show as 'TK' (Waitlist/Schedule change), use **`ERK`** to convert them to 'HK' (Hold Confirmed) status, followed by a **Receive From** entry to save the changes.

2. **Automated Involuntary Reissue (2:49):** Use the command **`FXI`** (Father X-ray India). This is the primary automated command to trigger an involuntary exchange, which generates a TST with a zero-fare difference (No ADC - Additional Collection).

3. **Managing Endorsements and Waiver Codes (3:14):** 
   * If the automatic endorsement is incorrect, use **`XE`** (e.g., `XE9`) to delete the specific line.
   * Retrieve the original endorsement using **`TWD/L8`** (where L8 is the line number of the ticket).
   * Combine the new waiver code provided by the airline with the original endorsement using a slash (e.g., `[New Waiver Code]/[Original Endorsement]`) (3:49).

4. **Updating OSI (Other Service Information) (4:10):** The waiver code must also be added to the OSI field using the format **`OS [Airline Code] [Waiver Code]`** (e.g., `OS LH FOC REISSUE DUE TO CREW STRIKE`).

5. **Finalizing the Exchange (4:30):**
   * Perform **`TQT`** to re-calculate the TST.
   * Execute **`TTPE`** followed by the TST number (e.g., `TTPE T8`) and **`RT`** to finalize the ticket exchange.
   * Verify the new ticket number using **`TWD`** (4:57).
   * To view the old ticket details (now in historical 'E' mode), use **`TWD O*`** (5:12).

**Important Tip:** Failure to include the airline-provided waiver code in both the Endorsement line and the OSI field can lead to an **ADM (Agency Debit Memo)** from the airline (5:53).
